Full-Book Closure Rule\n11\t\n12\tFor every major long-line setup, check whether the final answer is earned by prior scenes, clues, choices, and costs.\n13\t\n14\tReview these as a chain, not as isolated reveals:\n15\t\n16\t- first hook or warning;\n17\t- repeated clue or emotional echo;\n18\t- midpoint complication or false reading;\n19\t- character cost before the answer;\n20\t- final payoff;\n21\t- residue after the payoff.\n22\t\n23\tHard blocker: if a major long-line element is solved mainly by retrospective explanation, author summary, or a late new fact, the manuscript is below the final publication line even if the chapter prose is strong.\n24\t\n25\tFor mystery, dual-world, historical, transmigration, or evidence-chain novels, also ask:\n26\t\n27\t- Does every key object, document, wound, record, name, warning, or artifact change meaning more than once in a fair way?\n28\t- Can ordinary readers remember why it matters when it returns?\n29\t- Does the final meaning preserve earlier emotional truth, or does it cancel earlier scenes?\n30\t- Does the ending leave correct unanswered residue rather than unresolved plot debt?\n31\t\n32\t## 2. Continuous Reading Fatigue Rule\n33\t\n34\tBatch review must simulate a real reader reading several chapters in sequence. Do not judge each chapter only in isolation.\n35\t\n36\tCheck whether 3-5 consecutive chapters create fatigue through:\n37\t\n38\t- repeated institutional terms, document labels, or procedure language without enough scene pressure;\n39\t- multiple similar interrogation, report, or deduction scenes in a row;\n40\t- too many abstract summaries of stakes after readers already understand them;\n41\t- emotional intensity held at the same pitch without recovery, silence, or contrast;\n42\t- mystery density so high that readers understand each sentence but lose the larger direction.\n43\t\n44\tFatigue is not automatically a blocker. It becomes a release blocker when ordinary readers can no longer say what changed, who is in danger, or which question is now driving the story.\n45\t\n46\tPreferred fixes are usually small: vary scene type, move one explanation into action, add a human reaction beat, cut repeated labels, or make a chapter-ending question cleaner.\n47\t\n48\t## 3. Pre-Release Consistency Rule\n49\t\n50\tBefore declaring a manuscript or volume formally publishable, check continuity at the book level.\n51\t\n52\tReview:\n53\t\n54\t- character knowledge: what each major character knows, misunderstands, hides, and learns at each stage;\n55\t- naming: person names, titles, aliases, object names, evidence labels, chapter titles, and old/new readings;\n56\t- timeline: modern dates, ancient time markers, dream-entry/waking order, case sequence, and cause-effect order;\n57\t- evidence chain: who touched, hid, moved, sealed, read, damaged, restored, or reinterpreted each object;\n58\t- rules: dream-entry limits, return costs, legal/institutional constraints, professional permissions, and what can or cannot be tested;\n59\t- theme language: repeated key phrases should evolve in meaning, not merely repeat.\n60\t\n61\tHard blocker: if a continuity drift changes who knew a fact, when evidence became available, or whether a choice was possible, block final release until corrected.\n62\t\n63\t## 4. Final-Volume Emotional Conversion Rule\n64\t\n65\tA final volume should not only close accounts; it must convert answers into present-tense emotional pressure. Before declaring a final volume 爆款-ready, check whether each major reveal becomes a wound, danger, or irreversible choice.\n66\t\n67\tSpecifically inspect:\n68\t\n69\t- whether the final volume's system/procedure vocabulary overwhelms lived danger;\n70\t- whether the protagonist shows cumulative damage rather than isolated reaction tags;\n71\t- whether key allies, especially sacrificial allies, have private fear, desire, fatigue, or imperfection;\n72\t- whether every two or three chapters return to a plain human danger;\n73\t- whether chapter endings rely on cognitive summaries instead of actions, objects, silence, or changed relationships.\n74\t\n75\tIf these fail but structure and logic are sound, do not write `阻止发布`; write `不阻止发布，但不建议直接作为最终爆款稿`, then list the highest-yield emotional conversion fixes.\n76\t\n77\t## 5. Final Chapter Irreversibility Rule\n78\t\n79\tFor final publication or 爆款 review, inspect the last chapter separately after full-book closure. A final chapter should not merely confirm every old answer; it must show what the truth costs in the present.\n80\t\n81\tBefore passing the final chapter, require:\n82\t\n83\t- one central irreversible choice made on-page, not only a retrospective conclusion;\n84\t- visible aftershock on the protagonist's body, work permissions, speech, trust, or next ordinary action;\n85\t- at least one modern-line human cost when the modern line carries procedure, review, or institutional authority;\n86\t- restraint around emotional satisfaction when naming, clearing, or publishing the truth would undo a sacrifice or expose protected people;\n87\t- a final image or action that proves the protagonist has changed roles, not only learned the truth.\n88\t\n89\tIf the last chapter uses a sequence of acknowledgments, corrected labels, green checks, archive entries, report findings, or case-by-case vindications, verify that the sequence is broken by living pressure and not allowed to become a settlement list.\n90\t\n91\t## 6. Market-Facing Score Calibration Rule\n92\t\n93\tWhen the target is 爆款, do not inflate the score because the manuscript is complete, coherent, or clever. A third-party market score may be much lower than a strict craft score if the ending feels like system reconciliation rather than human pressure.\n94\t\n95\tUse these calibration anchors:\n96\t\n97\t- 90+ formal craft score can still be 70s-80s market score if readers admire the design but do not ache for people;\n98\t- high system/procedure density is a warning, not an automatic failure, but it must be offset by live cost in the same reading stretch;\n99\t- if the final review's harshest sentence is \"the book is already smart enough\", the next revision should not add more proof, rules, or explanation;\n100\t- for 爆款终稿, ask whether readers will say \"closed beautifully\" or \"I know this is right, but I cannot stand it.\" Prefer the second.\n101\t\n102\t### Gate Score Is Not Market Score\n103\t\n104\tFor final-publication reviews, never convert a formal gate pass into a 97+ external market score. A manuscript can pass release mechanics while still carrying blockbuster ceiling risk. Always separate:\n105\t\n106\t- formal publication gate verdict;\n107\t- external cold-read market score;\n108\t- residual ceiling risk after rules are satisfied.\n109\t\n110\tIf the final review mainly proves completion, closure, and correctness, but cannot name the live pain, changed behavior, or reader ache, do not call the manuscript a full blockbuster final draft. Write the softer verdict: `可发布，但不建议称为满血爆款终稿`.\n111\t## 7. Final-Chapter New-Case Re-Entry Rule\n112\t\n113\tIf a story ends by returning the protagonist to ordinary work, the return must show changed access, changed reflex, or changed self-understanding. A new case or new body at the end should not simply restore the original professional loop.\n114\t\n115\tCheck whether the final ordinary action proves change:\n116\t\n117\t- the protagonist cannot enter, sign, touch, or decide exactly as before;\n118\t- a permission table, role label, witness position, or colleague's intervention changes the workflow;\n119\t- the protagonist's first move toward the new dead is delayed, restrained, or rerouted by the cost of the finished case;\n120\t- the final gesture turns theme into behavior rather than a closing line.\n121\t\n122\tIf the ending says the protagonist has changed but the next professional action proceeds normally, downgrade final-chapter impact.\n123\t\n124\t## 8. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_113","type":"mcp_tool_call","server":"fastctx","tool":"read","arguments":{"file_path":"D:\\work\\other\\ai-writer\\.agents\\skills\\writer-review\\references\\page-turner-review.md","limit":280},"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"1\t# 陌生读者追读与爆款阅读感审稿规则\n2\t\n3\tApply `general-review.md` first. Use this file when reviewing long chapter batches, middle-to-late arcs, formal release candidates, stranger-reader feedback, reader stickiness, binge-read fatigue, commercial readability, or 爆款/追读/上头/陌生读者口径.\n4\t\n5\t## Core Purpose\n6\t\n7\tA manuscript can be logically complete and still feel cold. This module catches problems where the story is clever, coherent, and professionally textured, but ordinary readers begin to feel they are reading a system explanation rather than watching people under pressure.\n8\t\n9\tJudge this separately from basic publishability when useful:\n10\t\n11\t- Formal release line: structure, logic, fairness, prose, closure.\n12\t- Page-turner line: lived danger, emotional attachment, scene pressure, reader hunger, and whether answers become sharper conflict rather than tidy explanation.\n13\t\n14\tIf the user asks for 爆款感, 追读, 陌生第三方读者, or commercial readability, severe failures in this module are hard blockers for that goal even when the manuscript can technically publish.\n15\t\n16\t## 1. Concept Must Land In Scene Rule\n17\t\n18\tAbstract suspense terms must be anchored in visible pressure. When a scene advances through terms such as 证据链, 案牍, 复核, 报告, 屏幕, 待复核, 归类, 标注, 删改, 封存, 程序, or 权限, check whether the same scene also contains at least one concrete reader-gripping pressure:\n19\t\n20\t- a person is taken away, trapped, searched, questioned, silenced, exposed, or forced to choose;\n21\t- an object is seized, hidden, broken, sealed, moved, misread, or almost lost;\n22\t- a door, footstep, lamp, weapon, official hand, document, body, wound, or physical setting increases danger;\n23\t- a character leaks fear, hesitation, anger, shame, nausea, trembling, insomnia, appetite loss, or delayed shock;\n24\t- the abstract rule immediately changes who can live, speak, be believed, keep a name, keep a job, or stay out of custody.\n25\t\n26\tHard warning: if a climax happens mainly through explanation, screen display, report status, archival wording, or abstract inference, require scene pressure before calling it page-turner strong.\n27\t\n28\t## 2. Fourth-Quarter Clarity Rule\n29\t\n30\tFor late-middle arcs where the plot becomes conceptually sophisticated, especially arcs about drafts, original texts, annotations, official copies, reports, evidence chains, method misuse, or institutional classification, the review must repeatedly pull the problem back to the plain danger.\n31\t\n32\tEvery 2-3 chapters, an ordinary reader should be able to answer:\n33\t\n34\t- Who is about to die, be arrested, lose a name, lose credibility, be framed, or be erased?\n35\t- What will happen in the next scene if the protagonist is wrong?\n36\t- Which living person pays if this document, report, method, or record is mishandled?\n37\t- What does the antagonist gain now, not just in the abstract system?\n38\t\n39\tIf readers understand how the system works but stop fearing what it will do to someone immediately, the arc is too clever and too cold.\n40\t\n41\t## 3. Final-Arc Live Crisis Rule\n42\t\n43\tA final arc must not become a checklist of answers. Each reveal should function as a blade, not only a key.\n44\t\n45\tCheck whether every major answer increases a live crisis:\n46\t\n47\t- the protagonist loses authority, freedom, trust, access, identity, or professional position;\n48\t- an ally becomes easier to prosecute, erase, frame, or sacrifice;\n49\t- the love/trust relationship becomes harder, not merely clearer;\n50\t- the evidence that solves one mystery makes another person less safe;\n51\t- the final truth forces an irreversible choice in the present scene.\n52\t\n53\tHard blocker for final-arc page-turner review: if chapters primarily proceed as one reveal per chapter, with danger already over and only explanations remaining, mark the ending as settlement-list fatigue even if every answer is logical.\n54\t\n55\t## 4. Anti-Summary Sentence Rhythm Rule\n56\t\n57\tCognitive reversal sentences are powerful but must be rationed. Flag overuse of patterns such as:\n58\t\n59\t- 她终于明白...\n60\t- 她忽然明白...\n61\t- 她这才知道...\n62\t- 不是 X, 而是 Y...\n63\t- 这一次...\n64\t- 原来...\n65\t- 真正的...不是..., 是...\n66\t\n67\tThese can stay at major recognition points. At ordinary chapter turns, prefer action, object state, silence, body reaction, an unfinished line, or a changed relationship. Let readers arrive at the conclusion through scene pressure instead of having the narration summarize every turn.\n68\t\n69\tIf multiple consecutive chapter endings rely on explanatory recognition, downgrade pacing and prose texture.\n70\t\n71\t## 5. Protagonist Sticky Emotion Rule\n72\t\n73\tA capable protagonist must not only be admirable. Readers also need moments that make them worry for her, ache for her, or want to stay with her after the case logic is clear.\n74\t\n75\tFor high-pressure chapters, check for small private residue:\n76\t\n77\t- cannot sleep, cannot eat, forgets a message, avoids a call, washes hands too long, misses a familiar routine;\n78\t- hand tremor, nausea, cold fingertips, sore throat, delayed tears, anger that cannot be used;\n79\t- fear that professional judgment is becoming contamination;\n80\t- fear of becoming part of the same evidence system she is trying to challenge;\n81\t- one private sentence she cannot say aloud because it would break professional control.\n82\t\n83\tDo not add melodrama. The best residue is small, concrete, and behavior-changing.\n84\t\n85\t## 6. Important Male Lead / Ally Humanity Rule\n86\t\n87\tA sacrificial ally cannot only know, endure, withhold, and bear consequences. If a character repeatedly functions as protector, secret keeper, guide, or sacrifice device, require private human moments that do not only serve case mechanics.\n88\t\n89\tCheck for at least some of these across late arcs:\n90\t\n91\t- one selfish wish;\n92\t- one fear he does not want the protagonist to see;\n93\t- one moment of weakness, tiredness, hesitation, or resentment;\n94\t- one small ordinary preference or memory;\n95\t- one choice where he is not perfectly noble, but still human.\n96\t\n97\tIf the character always acts as fate's instrument, downgrade emotional impact of sacrifice.\n98\t\n99\t## 7. Modern-Line Human Cost Rule\n100\t\n101\tModern procedural characters should not only check, block, remind, report, or grant access. In a dual-world/procedural novel, modern-line people also need moments of agency and risk.\n102\t\n103\tCheck whether at least one modern ally, colleague, superior, or investigator:\n104\t\n105\t- bends a procedure and pays or risks a cost;\n106\t- protects the protagonist while still doubting her;\n107\t- chooses a hard official wording that changes the case;\n108\t- takes responsibility for a decision rather than only requesting more review;\n109\t- loses access, reputation, sleep, trust, or safety because of helping.\n110\t\n111\tIf modern characters only represent process, the modern line feels stable but emotionally thin.\n112\t\n113\t## 8. Blockbuster Final-Draft Dual Score Rule\n114\t\n115\tFor full manuscripts, final volumes, or any review where the target is 爆款/追读/上头, give two separate judgments:\n116\t\n117\t- `正式发布线`: can the manuscript be released without structural, logic, fairness, continuity, or prose blockers?\n118\t- `爆款追读线`: will a cold third-party reader keep reading because they fear for people, care about relationships, and feel each answer cut into a living person?\n119\t\n120\tDo not let a strong evidence chain inflate the page-turner score. A manuscript with complete logic but cold emotional delivery should be described as `不阻止发布，但不建议直接作为最终爆款稿`.\n121\t\n122\t## 9. Final-Volume System Density Gate\n123\t\n124\tFor final volumes, count or at least actively inspect system/procedure language such as 复核, 报告, 屏幕, 证据链, 案牍, 归类, 标注, 删改, 封存, 程序, 权限, 档案, 记录.\n125\t\n126\tHigh density is not automatically bad, but it becomes a page-turner risk when the dominant reading experience is `watching a system reconcile accounts` rather than `watching people get hurt, choose, lose, protect, or betray`.\n127\t\n128\tFor each final-volume reveal, require at least one immediate living-person cost:\n129\t\n130\t- someone loses access, credibility, name, job, safety, freedom, family protection, or legal standing;\n131\t- someone becomes easier to prosecute, erase, expose, frame, or sacrifice;\n132\t- someone must choose between truth and protecting another person;\n133\t- the evidence saves one dead person while endangering one living person;\n134\t- the protagonist's authority or self-image is damaged in a way that affects the next scene.\n135\t\n136\tIf a chapter can only be summarized as `the evidence chain became more complete`, mark the chapter below the 爆款追读线 even if it passes formal release.\n137\t\n138\t## 10. Continuous Protagonist Damage Rule\n139\t\n140\tA capable protagonist must show cumulative wear, not only isolated body reactions at plot nodes. For late arcs and final volumes, check whether the protagonist's pressure leaves behavioral residue across scenes:\n141\t\n142\t- avoids a phone call, message, doorway, file, name, or familiar place;\n143\t- sleeps badly, wakes with an old reflex, eats less, washes hands too long, or pauses before touching professional tools;\n144\t- cannot answer a family member, victim's relative, colleague, or ally directly;\n145\t- treats her own professional judgment as possible contamination;\n146\t- loses access, procedural authority, trust, or certainty, and that loss changes how she acts next.\n147\t\n148\tDo not ask for more explanatory inner monologue. The preferred fix is repeated small behavior that shows the case is wearing her down.\n149\t\n150\t## 11. Sacrificial Ally Non-Tool Gate\n151\t\n152\tFor a major ally or male lead who repeatedly knows, withholds, protects, sacrifices, or bears consequences, require human imperfection before the final sacrifice lands.\n153\t\n154\tAcross late arcs, look for at least one of:\n155\t\n156\t- a selfish wish;\n157\t- fear he does not want the protagonist to see;\n158\t- fatigue, resentment, hesitation, or a moment of wanting to stop;\n159\t- a small ordinary preference or memory unrelated to case mechanics;\n160\t- a choice that is protective but not perfectly noble;\n161\t- a moment where he almost lets someone else bear the cost.\n162\t\n163\tIf the character only functions as secret keeper, shield, guide, and sacrifice device, downgrade emotional impact even if the plot role is elegant.\n164\t\n165\t## 12. Every Two-to-Three Chapters Living-Danger Check\n166\t\n167\tIn concept-heavy arcs, every two or three chapters must let ordinary readers answer immediately:\n168\t\n169\t- Who may be arrested, erased, discredited, silenced, framed, or killed now?\n170\t- If the protagonist is wrong, who pays in the next scene?\n171\t- Which living person is endangered by preserving or exposing this evidence?\n172\t- What does the antagonist gain now, in concrete human terms?\n173\t\n174\tIf readers can only answer `the system/report/record changed`, the arc is too cold for 爆款追读.\n175\t\n176\t## 13. Explosive Final Chapter Gate\n177\t\n178\tFor novels targeting 爆款, 追读, 上头, or final market-facing release, the last chapter or final reveal chapter must not read like sequential case confirmation, catalog reconciliation, archive checkout, or a clean list of solved mysteries.\n179\t\n180\tA final chapter fails the 爆款追读线 if its dominant structure is: one old case is named, then another old case is named, then another old case is named, while the present-tense danger has already ended and no living person is forced to make a new irreversible choice.\n181\t\n182\tRequire at least one present-tense final-chapter pressure that ordinary readers can feel without understanding the whole evidence system:\n183\t\n184\t- the protagonist loses a real job function, permission, professional role, relationship, legal standing, or future access in the scene;\n185\t- a modern ally must sign, testify, be listed, accept inquiry, or otherwise put their own name under risk;\n186\t- a living person must remain protected by withholding a satisfying answer, name, confession, or public vindication;\n187\t- the protagonist must refuse an emotionally tempting fix because it would endanger people the sacrifice was meant to protect;\n188\t- the final action changes how the protagonist can work in the next ordinary case, not only how the old mystery is archived.\n189\t\n190\tHard warning: a beautiful, logical final chapter is still below 爆款终稿线 if readers mainly feel that every answer has been filed correctly. The final answer must also cost someone something now.\n191\t\n192\t## 14. Stop Proving Cleverness Gate\n193\t\n194\tFor 爆款 or market-facing reviews, do not let clever structure, perfect evidence-chain closure, or beautiful thematic symmetry hide a cold reading experience.\n195\t\n196\tFlag the manuscript when the reviewer's strongest praise is mainly \"the structure is self-consistent\", \"the evidence chain closes\", \"the system explains itself\", or \"the design is smart\", but it is hard to name who the reader is hurting for, angry for, or unwilling to leave.\n197\t\n198\tHard questions:\n199\t\n200\t- Does the evidence chain feel as if it wins by itself, or do people visibly pay for making it hold?\n201\t- Can a reader describe the final emotional wound without using technical words like 复核, 归档, 证据链, 标注, or 程序?\n202\t- Does each major answer make readers think \"this is correct, but I cannot stand what it costs\"?\n203\t- Is the final pleasure only intellectual closure, or also attachment, grief, unfairness, and reluctance to say goodbye?\n204\t\n205\tIf the book is already smart enough, the highest-yield review direction is usually not more explanation. Push the draft toward pain, reluctance, human imperfection, and immediate consequence.\n206\t\n207\t## 15. Continuous Professional Damage Chain\n208\t\n209\tA protagonist who is defined by a profession must not only suffer emotionally; the story should show how the case changes their professional reflexes.\n210\t\n211\tFor late arcs and final chapters, look for a chain of altered behavior, not isolated reaction tags:\n212\t\n213\t- the protagonist sees an original object or body and first restrains their hand;\n214\t- checks whether their name is in the correct permission/signature/role column before acting;\n215\t- no longer signs, touches, speaks, or promises in the old automatic way;\n216\t- enters a new ordinary case through a changed role, limited access, a witness position, or a delayed professional gesture;\n217\t- corrects or suppresses an old professional instinct because the case has made it dangerous.\n218\t\n219\tIf the manuscript only gives hand tremors, throat tightness, cold fingers, or one symbolic loss of authority, mark the professional damage as incomplete for 爆款终稿. The reader should feel that the protagonist cannot simply win and return to the same job-self.\n220\t\n221\t## 16. Imperfect Sacrifice Micro-Moment Gate\n222\t\n223\tA perfectly correct sacrifice earns respect; an imperfect sacrifice earns ache. For a major ally or male lead whose arc depends on taking blame, losing a name, withholding truth, or protecting others, require at least one brief humanly untidy moment near the sacrifice.\n224\t\n225\tAcceptable micro-moments include:\n226\t\n227\t- a flash of disappointment when the protagonist does not rescue or expose him;\n228\t- a selfish wish that she would choose him before choosing the safer truth;\n229\t- fatigue, resentment, or fear that leaks before he resumes control;\n230\t- a moment where he almost lets another person share the burden;\n231\t- a small ordinary desire for morning, food, rest, touch, a name, or being remembered;\n232\t- a response that is not noble first, but becomes restraint after a beat.\n233\t\n234\tDo not require melodramatic confession. The moment can be tiny, but it must prove he is not only a fate device, shield, or perfectly designed sacrifice.\n235\t\n236\t## 17. Modern Emotional Core Gate\n237\t\n238\tWhen a modern case or modern family member carries emotional ballast, do not let the result stop at \"疑点保留\", \"复核方向采纳\", or \"补充追问\". For 爆款追读, the chapter should also show what that limited procedural result means to a living person.\n239\t\n240\tCheck whether the scene gives ordinary readers a human handle:\n241\t\n242\t- a family member's breath, silence, hand, photo, repeated question, or inability to celebrate;\n243\t- the protagonist's inability to promise a result even after preserving the question;\n244\t- a modern ally taking responsibility for keeping the question alive;\n245\t- a clear sense that the win is partial, unsatisfying, and still necessary.\n246\t\n247\tIf the modern emotional core only produces a cleaner report status, mark it as too cold even if the procedure is correct.\n248\t\n249\t## 18. Review Output Add-On\n250\t\n251\tWhen this module applies, include a short page-turner judgment. For full manuscripts or final volumes, include both formal release and blockbuster/page-turner final-draft judgments:\n252\t\n253\t1. Formal release verdict: pass / block / not applicable.\n254\t2. 爆款追读 verdict: pass / caution / blocker.\n255\t3. Concept density: pass / caution / blocker.\n256\t4. Live scene pressure: pass / caution / blocker.\n257\t5. Character stickiness: pass / caution / blocker.\n258\t6. Final-arc crisis: pass / caution / blocker, if relevant.\n259\t7. One or two highest-yield fixes.\n260\t\n261\tDo not confuse a cold-but-logical manuscript with a broken manuscript. State whether the issue blocks formal publication, blocks page-turner/commercial goal, or is only a polishing opportunity.\n262\t## 9. External Cold-Read Score Rule\n263\t\n264\tFor 爆款/追读/上头 reviews, do not reuse the internal publication-gate score as the page-turner market score. The page-turner score is a stranger-reader risk estimate: it asks whether readers care, fear, ache, and continue, not merely whether the manuscript satisfies craft rules.\n265\t\n266\tUse this scale:\n267\t\n268\t- 95+: readers are likely to feel strong hunger and emotional attachment; call it strong page-turner pass.\n269\t- 90-94: page-turner pass with remaining ceiling risk.\n270\t- 85-89: publishable and promising, but not a final blockbuster draft.\n271\t- 80-84: formal publication may pass, but market stickiness is unstable.\n272\t- Below 80: unsuitable for a blockbuster target without major market-facing revision.\n273\t\n274\tIf the best praise is \"closed loop, clever evidence, complete rules\", with no clear answer to \"who will readers hurt for\", withhold 90+. {"type":"item.completed","item":{"id":"item_114","type":"mcp_tool_call","server":"fastctx","tool":"read","arguments":{"file_path":"D:\\work\\other\\ai-writer\\.agents\\skills\\writer-review\\references\\psychology-readability.md","limit":260},"result":{"content":[{"type":"text","text":"1\t# 心理描写与普通读者可读性硬规则\n2\t\n3\tApply `general-review.md` first. Use this file when the user asks about readability, psychological detail, ordinary-reader understanding, professionality versus clarity, or when major identity/danger/emotional moments appear.\n4\t\n5\t## Ordinary Reader Rule\n6\t\n7\tProfessional writing does not mean obscure writing. Let expert readers feel accuracy and ordinary readers grasp danger.\n8\t\n9\tAcross a high-stakes sequence, readers should be able to follow the character's response. Check the following where relevant; no single paragraph must carry them all:\n10\t\n11\t- what triggered the reaction;\n12\t- what the body or behavior gives away, if anything;\n13\t- what old knowledge, habit, or memory is pulled in, if relevant;\n14\t- what current danger invalidates easy explanations;\n15\t- what choice the character makes next.\n16\t\n17\t## Major Psychological Scene Standard\n18\t\n19\tMajor psychological nodes cannot be solved by one or two explanatory sentences. They need to read like a scene, not a patch.\n20\t\n21\tFor nodes carrying opening retention, identity shock, institutional danger, betrayal, evidence reversal, or a chapter hook, use the following beats as diagnostic lenses. Select and order those the character and scene need; do not fill all eight into every scene or give different characters the same sequence:\n22\t\n23\t1. External trigger: sound, object, touch, name, phrase, wound, document, or action hits first.\n24\t2. Body or behavioral reaction: a hand, breath, sightline, step, routine, or deliberate stillness may register pressure before explanation.\n25\t3. Old-world return: memory, job habit, prior case, family, training, or earlier scene flashes back.\n26\t4. Reality pressure: concrete present details prove this cannot be hand-waved.\n27\t5. Cognitive testing: the character tries one explanation, then it fails.\n28\t6. Keyword pressure: a name, legal term, object, title, or phrase repeats and gains weight.\n29\t7. Delayed emotion: fear, shame, shock, grief, or anger arrives after confusion or professional control.\n30\t8. Action choice: silence, retreat, question, refuse, remember, record, hide, follow, or confront.\n31\t\n32\tIf readers only know “she was shocked/alert/sad” but cannot feel how this particular person got there, the scene is thin. A restrained scene may carry that process through an omission, a changed question, a failed routine, or one costly choice rather than a long internal monologue.\n33\t\n34\t## Abnormal Entry Rule\n35\t\n36\tFor first dream-entry, transmigration, time-slip, or impossible awakening, include at least:\n37\t\n38\t- lost modern anchors: phone, ID, notebook, familiar clothing, lights, workplace;\n39\t- sensory reality: cold, wet, smell, pressure, pain, sound, texture;\n40\t- world mismatch: unfamiliar language, architecture, clothing, tool, rule, title;\n41\t- knowledge trigger: professional or lived knowledge connects the danger;\n42\t- no safe test: she cannot risk treating it as a dream;\n43\t- survival switch: she chooses the least dangerous action.\n44\t\n45\t## Concrete Consequence Rule\n46\t\n47\tInstitutional, legal, historical, or professional danger must be written as a concrete consequence.\n48\t\n49\tPrefer clear outcomes:\n50\t\n51\t- be taken away for questioning;\n52\t- be written into a charge;\n53\t- be beaten, detained, isolated, silenced, misidentified, or removed from records;\n54\t- lose access, credibility, identity, custody, evidence, or witness protection.\n55\t\n56\tAvoid condensed phrases that require translation when stakes should be instant.\n57\t\n58\t## Novel, Not Screenplay\n59\t\n60\tDo not reduce review to camera movement, dialogue, and blocking. A novel scene also needs:\n61\t\n62\t- internal pressure;\n63\t- narrative selection;\n64\t- cognition under stress;\n65\t- sensory continuity;\n66\t- sentence rhythm matching mental state;\n67\t- consequences that outlast the visible action.\n68\t\n69\t## Professional Clarity Rule\n70\t\n71\tMaking prose easier for ordinary readers should not lower professionality. Keep professionality in evidence, process, procedure, and constraint; make danger, emotion, and choice immediately understandable.\n72\t\n73\t## Authentic Human Reaction Rule\n74\t\n75\tExtreme scenes must simulate real human psychology, not only a plot-useful emotional summary. A character under shock, danger, grief, betrayal, institutional pressure, or impossible evidence should not react like a clean reasoning engine.\n76\t\n77\tCheck whether the reaction includes human friction:\n78\t\n79\t- involuntary body response before clean thought: breath, hands, throat, stomach, vision, skin, balance, temperature, pain;\n80\t- attention narrowing or misdirection: the character notices one concrete, sometimes irrational detail before understanding the whole event;\n81\t- failed normal habit: reaching for a phone, badge, pocket, tool, familiar procedure, familiar person, or old routine that no longer works;\n82\t- delayed comprehension: the character does not instantly understand the full meaning; the realization arrives in steps;\n83\t- self-protection or avoidance: denial, bargaining, silence, checking small facts, not wanting to look, not wanting to say a name, or choosing a smaller immediate goal;\n84\t- contradiction: professional control and human fear can coexist; love, suspicion, shame, anger, and duty may pull against each other;\n85\t- consequence after the moment: the reaction changes later speech, trust, body movement, memory, or decision.\n86\t\n87\tIf the text jumps directly from event to correct analysis, thematic statement, or efficient decision, check for AI-like psychological writing. Mark it only when the character lacks an established reason for that response and the scene gives no meaningful leak, friction, or later consequence; a practiced professional can act quickly without becoming emotionless.\n88\t\n89\t## Anti-AI Psychology Checklist\n90\t\n91\tFlag a scene when any of these appear in a major psychological node:\n92\t\n93\t- The character names the emotion but the reader cannot feel how it formed.\n94\t- The character processes danger in a perfectly linear sequence: observe, infer, decide, with no bodily lag or emotional interference.\n95\t- The reaction mainly explains plot stakes to the reader instead of showing what the character can bear, avoid, misunderstand, or choose.\n96\t- Every character has the same calm, compressed, analytical response style.\n97\t- The scene uses elegant abstract lines where a human would first have messy sensory, bodily, or practical reactions.\n98\t- The character instantly accepts an impossible event, betrayal, death, or institutional threat without testing, denial, stunned silence, or partial misreading.\n99\t- A supposedly devastating moment leaves no residue in later movement, speech, trust, sleep, appetite, attention, or decision.\n100\t\n101\tFor publication review, a major node with AI-like psychology is a hard blocker even if the plot logic is sound. Score it below the publication line until the scene is rebuilt as lived human experience.\n102\t\n103\t## Supporting Character Humanity Rule\n104\t\n105\tPsychological authenticity is not a protagonist-only standard. When reviewing or polishing a scene, check the emotional reality of every character who changes the pressure of the scene.\n106\t\n107\tSupporting characters can be concise, but they still need human logic:\n108\t\n109\t- a witness may hide facts because speaking will cost work, marriage, safety, or family;\n110\t- a servant may obey cruelty because the household controls food, debt, kin, or shelter;\n111\t- an official may follow procedure because a wrong record can destroy their post;\n112\t- an antagonist may sound calm because they have rehearsed the moral excuse that lets them live with harm;\n113\t- a victim may cooperate, lie, delay, or refuse rescue because someone else will pay if they act freely;\n114\t- a colleague may doubt the protagonist because their own professional duty demands doubt.\n115\t\n116\tDuring polishing, do not merely make these characters more vivid with description. Give them pressure-specific behavior: what they protect, what they avoid saying, what detail they notice first, what they risk by helping, what they gain by harming, and how their body or speech leaks their position.\n117\t\n118\tIf a scene's emotional truth depends on a supporting character, that character must not be reduced to a reaction button for the protagonist.\n119\t\n120\t## Character-Specific Psychology Rule\n121\t\n122\tPsychological detail must be character-specific, not generic human fear pasted onto the plot. Before judging a major emotional or danger scene, identify the character's baseline from `general-review.md`: profession, training, status, wound, fear, relationship pressure, self-protection style, and usual speech/attention pattern.\n123\t\n124\tThen check whether the psychological beats are customized:\n125\t\n126\t- a professional may first reach for procedure, evidence, tools, record, diagnosis, chain of custody, or risk control, but the body should still leak fear or shock;\n127\t- a powerless or low-status character may first scan authority, punishment, witnesses, exits, speech taboos, and who can be blamed;\n128\t- a controlling character may withhold, redirect, ask questions, or manage the room before admitting feeling;\n129\t- a protective character may notice who is exposed before noticing their own pain;\n130\t- a guilty character may misread neutral details as accusation;\n131\t- a suspicious character may treat help as danger until trust is earned;\n132\t- an impulsive character may act before understanding, then pay for the mistake.\n133\t\n134\tDo not accept the same reaction style for everyone: calm analysis, neat inner monologue, perfect inference, and clean emotional labels create AI-like sameness. The review should ask what this person would miss, distort, suppress, or over-focus on because of who they already are.\n135\t\n136\tFor a consequential departure from an established baseline, check three kinds of evidence:\n137\t\n138\t1. a visible trigger strong enough to crack the old pattern;\n139\t2. a bodily, verbal, relational, or tactical cost;\n140\t3. residue in later behavior, trust, speech, sleep, attention, or choices.\n141\t\n142\tAn arc-turning change usually needs all three across the scene and its aftermath; a smaller departure may be justified by a clear trigger and one visible cost or residue. Do not add inner exposition solely to fill this list. If a pivotal reversal has no support, it reads as author convenience rather than character change.\n143\t\n144\t\n145\t## Chapter-Level Psychological Gate\n146\t\n147\tEvery chapter that contains psychological description, emotional reaction, fear, shock, grief, suspicion, desire, hesitation, guilt, trust, betrayal, institutional pressure, or character choice must be reviewed against the character's established baseline. This is not limited to the protagonist.\n148\t\n149\tBefore passing the chapter, identify for each important psychological node:\n150\t\n151\t- whose mind/body is under pressure;\n152\t- that character's known personality, profession, status, fear, wound, duty, relationship pressure, and default coping style;\n153\t- what this specific character would notice first;\n154\t- what ordinary habit, professional reflex, social reflex, or self-protection reflex would fail or take over;\n155\t- how the reaction changes the next line, movement, speech, trust, or decision.\n156\t\n157\tA chapter does not pass this gate if psychological writing is merely a small sentence patch such as adding one body reaction, one thought, or one abstract emotional label. For any meaningful psychological node, require a character-specific causal sequence. Major nodes need enough of the diagnostic beats above to make the response felt and the choice understandable; smaller nodes may be concise, using a trigger and a character-specific perception, omission, action, or residue. Do not require the same number or order of beats from every character.\n158\t\n159\tWhen reviewing a batch, do not say \"minor polish\" if several chapters contain generic psychology. Mark it as a publication risk or blocker depending on severity. The expected revision direction is not \"add more inner monologue\"; it is to rebuild perception order, sentence rhythm, bodily behavior, failed habit, and later consequence so the scene feels like this person, not a competent generic narrator.\n160\t\n161\tHard blocker: if a chapter's important psychological moment could belong to any character after changing the name, or if it reads like one or two explanatory sentences added after the plot was already built, the chapter is below the publication line until that node is rebuilt as character-bound lived experience.\n162\t## Psychological Polishing Rule\n163\t\n164\tFor polishing, the target is not \"add more inner monologue.\" The target is to rebuild the moment so the reader can feel a specific human mind under pressure.\n165\t\n166\tA psychological polish should usually change several of these, not just add one explanatory sentence:\n167\t\n168\t- external trigger: make the object, sound, name, wound, rule, or gesture hit the character first;\n169\t- bodily leak: add or sharpen the involuntary reaction before clean analysis;\n170\t- failed habit: let the character reach for a familiar tool, phrase, role, procedure, or relationship that no longer works;\n171\t- partial misunderstanding: let comprehension arrive late or wrong before it becomes accurate;\n172\t- character-specific focus: choose details this person would over-notice because of profession, fear, guilt, status, love, or suspicion;\n173\t- sentence rhythm: shorten, fragment, delay, repeat, or withhold according to the mental state;\n174\t- choice under limit: end with a small survivable action instead of a complete explanation;\n175\t- aftershock: let the polished moment affect the next exchange, movement, trust, or decision.\n176\t\n177\tA polished paragraph should pass this test: if the name is removed, the reaction still points to this character and not to any competent protagonist.\n178\t\n179\t## Extreme Situation Simulation Standard\n180\t\n181\tFor life-threatening, identity-breaking, impossible, grief-heavy, or betrayal scenes, the review should ask:\n182\t\n183\t1. What would this specific person notice first, before they understand the event?\n184\t2. What normal habit would fail in this situation?\n185\t3. What bodily reaction arrives before language?\n186\t4. What wrong or incomplete explanation would they try first?\n187\t5. What concrete detail makes denial impossible?\n188\t6. What emotion arrives late, after confusion or control?\n189\t7. What small action do they choose because they cannot solve the whole crisis yet?\n190\t8. How does this moment alter the next scene, not just this paragraph?\n191\t\n192\tA strong revision should not merely add more inner monologue. It should change perception order, sentence rhythm, body behavior, and later choices so the reader feels a human mind trying to survive the moment.\n193\t\n\n(Complete: reached end of file; lines 1-193 of 193 shown.)"}],"structured_content":null},"error":null,"status":"completed"}}
